hijackscan: 100 domains by risk

Per-domain risk of certificate theft through BGP hijacks below DNS

How to read this page.

One row per domain, worst first: severity (CVSS scale), score 0–10, label (what drives the score), what an attacker can do, and the single fix that lowers the score most. Click a row for the calculation, every attack path, the findings with evidence, what each fix does, the CVSS vectors and the ATT&CK techniques. Formula: design/formula.md. Source: data/out/ee/domains.jsonl.

Critical10.03a.eeopen-webA forged more-specific route to the web server of 3a.ee gets a certificate from any CA (1 of 1 addresses forgeable; 100% success in 5 attempts).
fix first: domain-owner: publish CAA issue=<your CA>; accounturi=<your ACME account> → 1.1 Low

What to do

owner: action → score after
fix firstdomain-owner: publish CAA issue=<your CA>; accounturi=<your ACME account> → 1.1 Low
to closedomain-owner + dns-host: publish CAA issue=<your CA>; accounturi=<your ACME account> + sign the zone and publish the DS at the registrar (DNSSEC) → 0.0 None

How the score was calculated

driving path: web → 3a.ee
p = 1.00   (1 of 1 addresses forgeable)
P per attempt = p × P(≥ 3 of 4 remote perspectives fooled) = 1.000   [CA/B BR 3.2.2.9]
L = 1 − (1 − P)^5 = 1.000
K = 0.87^0 = 1.000
score = Roundup(10 × 1 × K × L) = 10.0 → Critical (CVSS scale)

All attack paths

pathtargetresultphwhy
web3a.eeCritical 10.01.0001 of 1 addresses forgeable
webwww.3a.eeCritical 10.01.0001 of 1 addresses forgeable
contestweb + nameserversLow 1.10.301exact-prefix origin contest, always possible without ASPA (assumed share)

Findings

  • WEB_MORE_SPECIFIC Critical exposure — 3a.ee: 1 of 1 addresses forgeable
    • 185.26.106.234 in 185.26.106.0/23: no ROA, a forged more-specific is never RPKI-invalid
  • WEB_MORE_SPECIFIC Critical exposure — www.3a.ee: 1 of 1 addresses forgeable
    • 185.26.106.234 in 185.26.106.0/23: no ROA, a forged more-specific is never RPKI-invalid
  • EXACT_PREFIX_CONTEST Low exposure — exact-prefix origin contest, always possible without ASPA (assumed share)
  • NO_ROA exposure — 1 addresses not covered by any ROA
    • 185.26.106.234 in 185.26.106.0/23: no ROA, a forged more-specific is never RPKI-invalid
  • NO_CAA missing-defence — no CAA record at any label up to the TLD: every CA may issue
  • UNSIGNED missing-defence — no DS at the parent: DNS answers can be forged

What each fix does (re-scored)

score afterseverityowneraction
0.0Nonedomain-owner + dns-hostpublish CAA issue=<your CA>; accounturi=<your ACME account> + sign the zone and publish the DS at the registrar (DNSSEC)
1.1Lowdomain-ownerpublish CAA issue=<your CA>; accounturi=<your ACME account>
1.1Lowhosting-providerset ROA maxLength = announced length for the web prefixes
10.0Criticaldns-hostsign the zone and publish the DS at the registrar (DNSSEC)
10.0Criticaldns-hostset ROA maxLength = announced length for the nameserver prefixes

Vectors

CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:C/C:H/I:H/A:N base 10.0 (CVSS alone: same for every domain on this path)
CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:N/VC:N/VI:N/VA:N/SC:H/SI:H/SA:N

MITRE ATT&CK

  • T1590.005 Gather Victim Network Information: IP Addresses
  • T1596 Search Open Technical Databases (BGP, RPKI)
  • T1584.008 Compromise Infrastructure: Network Devices (a BGP-speaking network)
  • T1557 Adversary-in-the-Middle (closest fit: ATT&CK has no BGP hijack technique)
  • T1588.004 Obtain Capabilities: Digital Certificates
  • T1608.003 Stage Capabilities: Install Digital Certificate

  • WEB_MORE_SPECIFIC Critical exposure — www.84k.ee: 1 of 1 addresses forgeable

  • NS_CAPTURABLE Low exposure — 2 of 27 nameserver addresses forgeable
    • 125.94.59.155 in 125.88.0.0/13: no ROA, a forged more-specific is never RPKI-invalid
    • 49.7.107.155 in 49.7.64.0/18: no ROA, a forged more-specific is never RPKI-invalid
  • EXACT_PREFIX_CONTEST Low exposure — exact-prefix origin contest, always possible without ASPA (assumed share)
  • NO_ROA exposure — 2 addresses not covered by any ROA
    • 125.94.59.155 in 125.88.0.0/13: no ROA, a forged more-specific is never RPKI-invalid
    • 49.7.107.155 in 49.7.64.0/18: no ROA, a forged more-specific is never RPKI-invalid
  • NO_CAA missing-defence — no CAA record at any label up to the TLD: every CA may issue
  • UNSIGNED missing-defence — no DS at the parent: DNS answers can be forged

Low2.003183.eens-partial6 of 16 nameserver addresses forgeable: forged answers reach enough CA perspectives in 25% of hijacks (5 attempts each).
fix first: dns-host: sign the zone and publish the DS at the registrar (DNSSEC) → 1.1 Low

What to do

owner: action → score after
fix firstdns-host: sign the zone and publish the DS at the registrar (DNSSEC) → 1.1 Low
to closedomain-owner + dns-host: publish CAA issue=<your CA>; accounturi=<your ACME account> + sign the zone and publish the DS at the registrar (DNSSEC) → 0.0 None

How the score was calculated

driving path: ns → nameservers
p = 0.38   (6 of 16 nameserver addresses forgeable)
P per attempt = p × P(≥ 3 of 4 remote perspectives fooled) = 0.057   [CA/B BR 3.2.2.9]
L = 1 − (1 − P)^5 = 0.254
K = 0.87^2 = 0.757   (attacker must run a forged authoritative DNS server; 3 prefixes must be hijacked at once)
score = Roundup(10 × 1 × K × L) = 2.0 → Low (CVSS scale)

How the score was calculated

driving path: ns → nameservers
p = 0.33   (2 of 6 nameserver addresses forgeable)
P per attempt = p × P(≥ 3 of 4 remote perspectives fooled) = 0.037   [CA/B BR 3.2.2.9]
L = 1 − (1 − P)^5 = 0.172
K = 0.87^2 = 0.757   (attacker must run a forged authoritative DNS server; 2 prefixes must be hijacked at once)
score = Roundup(10 × 1 × K × L) = 1.4 → Low (CVSS scale)

How the score was calculated

driving path: contest → web + nameservers
p = 0.30   (exact-prefix origin contest, always possible without ASPA (assumed share))
P per attempt = p × P(≥ 3 of 4 remote perspectives fooled) = 0.025   [CA/B BR 3.2.2.9]
L = 1 − (1 − P)^5 = 0.119
K = 0.87^1 = 0.870   (wins only where the attacker's route is preferred)
score = Roundup(10 × 1 × K × L) = 1.1 → Low (CVSS scale)

How the score was calculated

driving path: contest → web + nameservers
p = 0.30   (exact-prefix origin contest, always possible without ASPA (assumed share))
P per attempt = p × P(≥ 3 of 4 remote perspectives fooled) = 0.025   [CA/B BR 3.2.2.9]
L = 1 − (1 − P)^5 = 0.119
K = 0.87^1 = 0.870   (wins only where the attacker's route is preferred)
score = Roundup(10 × 1 × K × L) = 1.1 → Low (CVSS scale)

  • UNSIGNED missing-defence — no DS at the parent: DNS answers can be forged

How the score was calculated

driving path: contest → web + nameservers
p = 0.30   (exact-prefix origin contest, always possible without ASPA (assumed share))
P per attempt = p × P(≥ 3 of 4 remote perspectives fooled) = 0.025   [CA/B BR 3.2.2.9]
L = 1 − (1 − P)^5 = 0.119
K = 0.87^1 = 0.870   (wins only where the attacker's route is preferred)
score = Roundup(10 × 1 × K × L) = 1.1 → Low (CVSS scale)

    • 2803:f800:50::6ca2:c0d7 in 2803:f800:50::/45: ROA for 2803:f800:50::/47→48 inside it makes that more-specific RPKI-valid

How the score was calculated

driving path: contest → web + nameservers
p = 0.30   (exact-prefix origin contest, always possible without ASPA (assumed share))
P per attempt = p × P(≥ 3 of 4 remote perspectives fooled) = 0.025   [CA/B BR 3.2.2.9]
L = 1 − (1 − P)^5 = 0.119
K = 0.87^1 = 0.870   (wins only where the attacker's route is preferred)
score = Roundup(10 × 1 × K × L) = 1.1 → Low (CVSS scale)

    • 2803:f800:50::6ca2:c185 in 2803:f800:50::/45: ROA for 2803:f800:50::/47→48 inside it makes that more-specific RPKI-valid
  • EXACT_PREFIX_CONTEST Low exposure — exact-prefix origin contest, always possible without ASPA (assumed share)
  • NO_CAA missing-defence — no CAA record at any label up to the TLD: every CA may issue
  • UNSIGNED missing-defence — no DS at the parent: DNS answers can be forged
